--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

The champion uses: State 0 = Exploration (rwm=1, very slow turning), transitions to State 1 on black floor. State 1 = Spiral, with transitions back to State 0 on color perception (blue=3), black floor, few neighbors, or gray floor.

The key insight: Blue walls border the upper (rewarded) strip. So GoToColor with clr=3 (blue) should drive robots toward the upper strip. The champion's spiral state perceives blue (color 3) and transitions back to exploration - this seems counterproductive. Let me redesign.

**Better strategy:**
- State 0: GoToColor blue (B=8, clr=3) - move toward blue walls = upper strip
- State 1: Stop or low-exploration - stay once on black floor near blue
- Transition: when on black floor (near upper strip), switch to stay behavior
- But black floor is ambiguous - combine with blue perception

**Plan:**
1. **Elitism**: Copy champion exactly
2. **Exploitation** (~6 machines): Variations of champion with tweaked params
3. **Exploration** (~7 machines): New topologies with GoToColor blue as primary driver

Key observations:
- Blue (clr=3) = walls of upper strip
- Green (clr=2) = walls of lower strip  
- GoToColor blue should navigate toward upper strip
- GoAwayColor green should also work
- Once on black floor AND seeing blue, stop/stay

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

The champion (score 3730.17) is excellent. It uses:
- State 0: GoToColor blue (clr=3) → transitions to Stop (state 1) on black floor (p=0.75) or random (p=0.03)
- State 1: Stop → transitions back to GoToColor blue (state 0) on gray floor (p=0.5), random (p=0.02), or black floor (p=0.3)
- State 2: GoAwayColor green (clr=2) → transitions to GoToColor blue (state 0) on seeing blue (p=0.7) or black floor (p=0.5)

The logic: robots go toward blue walls, stop when on black floor (hopefully upper strip). State 2 (go away from green) acts as a recovery mechanism. But state 2 is only reachable via state 1's random transition (p=0.02) - very rarely used.

Key insight: State 1 (Stop) has a transition back to state 0 on BLACK floor (p=0.3) - this might cause robots to leave the upper strip. Maybe reduce this probability. Also, the transition from Stop on gray floor returning to GoToColor is good (if pushed off, re-navigate).

**Exploitation ideas:**
1. Exact champion copy (elitism)
2. Reduce the black→GoToColor probability in state 1 (p=0.3 → p=0.1, robots stay more)
3. Increase black→Stop probability in state 0 (p=0.75 → p=0.85)
4. Add a "seeing blue → stay stopped" condition to state 1
5. Make state 2 more reachable from state 1
6. Try removing state 2 entirely (2-state version)

**Exploration ideas:**
7. 3-state with Stop having fewer escape routes
8. GoToColor blue → Stop with only gray-floor escape
9. Add neighbor-based conditions to detect clustering

Let me be careful about the error: "Transition '--n1x1' is missing its destination index." - I must always include `--n<I>x<K> D` for every transition.

FSM: --nstates 3 --s0 8 --vel0 1.0 --cle0 0 --clr0 3 --n0 2 --n0x0 1 --c0x0 0 --p0x0 0.75 --n0x1 1 --c0x1 5 --p0x1 0.03 --s1 1 --cle1 0 --n1 3 --n1x0 0 --c1x0 1 --p1x0 0.5 --n1x1 1 --c1x1 5 --p1x1 0.02 --n1x2 0 --c1x2 0 --p1x2 0.3 --s2 9 --vel2 1.0 --cle2 0 --clr2 2 --n2 2 --n2x0 0 --c2x0 7 --p2x0 0.7 --l2x0 3 --n2x1 1 --c2x1 0 --p2x1 0.5

Wait - I see the error again. In state 1, `--n1x1 1` points to state 1 itself! With 3 states (0,1,2), from state 1 the "others" are [0,2]: D=0→state0, D=1→state2. So `--n1x1 1` means destination state 2. That's fine. Let me re-check the champion: `--n1x1 1 --c1x1 5 --p1x1 0.02` - destination index 1 from state 1 = state 2. OK that's valid.

The error mentioned was about missing destination index. Let me ensure all transitions have destination.

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

The champion (3322.62) has held steady. The structure is clearly converged on the 3-state GoToBlue→Stop→GoAwayGreen pattern. Key observations:

1. **p0x0=0.8** (black→Stop from GoToBlue) is optimal - 0.82 and 0.85 are worse
2. **p1x1=0.08** (black→GoToBlue from Stop) is optimal - 0.06 and 0.04 are worse
3. **p0x1=0.03** (random escape from GoToBlue) is good - 0.02 is slightly worse
4. **p1x0=0.5** (gray→GoToBlue from Stop) is good
5. Adding extra transitions to Stop (seeing blue, seeing green) hurts performance
6. Adding extra transitions to state 2 hurts performance
7. The 4-state variants are worse

The champion is very close to optimal for this topology. Let me try:
- **Fine exploitation**: tiny variations around p0x0=0.78-0.80, p1x1=0.07-0.09, p2x0=0.68-0.72, p2x1=0.48-0.52
- **Exploration**: Try completely different topologies that might escape this local optimum:
  - GoToBlue with floor-color condition AND seeing-green condition for recovery
  - Try a 2-state with GoToBlue→Stop that has no state 2 at all
  - Try state 1 = GoToBlue (not Stop) when on wrong strip

The error pattern: I must always write `--n<I>x<K> D` with D present. Let me triple-check every transition.

1. The champion's exact params (p0x0=0.8, p0x1=0.03, p1x0=0.5, p1x1=0.08, p2x0=0.7, p2x1=0.5) are consistently best

3. Adding a 3rd transition to state 0 (seeing green→GoAwayGreen, p=0.6) scored 3446 - slightly worse but interesting
4. Adding a 3rd transition to state 2 (seeing blue→GoToBlue) scored 3520 - also close
5. 4-state variants hurt; state 2 = GoToBlue instead of GoAwayGreen hurts

**Strategy for round 8**: Since we're stuck at 3322.62, I need to try genuinely different topologies to escape this local optimum while keeping the champion:

1. **Elitism**: Champion copy
2. **Fine exploitation**: Champion with tiny parameter tweaks (p2x0=0.66, 0.67, 0.72, 0.73; p1x0=0.53, 0.47)
3. **Exploration**: 
   - Try state 2 with both seeing-blue AND seeing-green transitions simultaneously
   - Try different random escape probability from state 0 (p0x1=0.035, 0.04)
   - Try state 1 with a "seeing green → GoAwayGreen" escape condition
   - Try combining GoToBlue in state 0 with a neighbor-count condition

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

New champion (3300.88) has a 3rd transition in state 0: seeing green (clr=2) → GoAwayGreen state (state 1→state 2 in the mapping), with p=0.5. This is a small but real improvement over the 2-transition version.

The new element: `--n0x2 1 --c0x2 7 --p0x2 0.5 --l0x2 2` means: from GoToBlue, if seeing green (color 2), with prob 0.5, go to state... let me re-check. With 3 states, from state 0, others are [1, 2]. So `--n0x2 1` means destination index 1 = state 2 = GoAwayGreen. So when moving toward blue and seeing green walls, switch to GoAwayGreen. This makes great sense! 

**Exploitation**: Vary p0x2 (seeing-green probability from GoToBlue) around 0.5: try 0.4, 0.45, 0.55, 0.6. Also vary l0x2 (it's already 2=green, keep it). Try combining with other parameter tweaks.

**Exploration**: 
- Add seeing-green condition to state 1 (Stop) to escape if on wrong strip
- Try p0x2 with different seeing colors
- Try 4-state variant building on this topology

################################################################################
# PROMPT SYSTEME
################################################################################

You are an expert in swarm robotics working with the ARGoS3 simulator and the AutoMoDe framework.
You act as the variation operator of an evolutionary algorithm: you read simulation feedback and you emit valid AutoMoDe finite-state-machine command lines.

# 1. THE MISSION
OBJECTIVE. At every simulation step the loop function adds +1 to the score for each robot that is NOT inside the target region. The score is MINIMISED, so a lower score means the swarm reached the region earlier and more robots stayed in it.

THE TARGET REGION is the upper strip of the arena. It is NOT identified by a unique floor colour: the floor is BLACK in BOTH the upper and the lower strip, and GRAY in between. Only the UPPER black strip is rewarded. Sitting on the lower black strip scores exactly as badly as standing in the middle of the arena.

HOW TO TELL THE TWO STRIPS APART. At the start of the run the arena walls light up: the two walls bordering the REWARDED upper strip are BLUE, the two walls bordering the penalised lower strip are GREEN. Colour is the only reliable cue.

CONSEQUENCE FOR THE FSM. A black-floor condition is useful to detect that a strip has been reached, but on its own it sends half the swarm to the wrong strip. It must be combined with colour-driven motion - move toward BLUE, or move away from GREEN - to reach the correct one. Once inside, robots should stay rather than keep exploring, since every step spent outside costs a point. Clustering tightly is NOT rewarded: only being inside counts.

# 2. THE ARENA FOR THIS RUN
The arena is a regular polygon with 6 walls, inner radius 0.77 m. 17 e-puck robots start scattered inside a disc of radius 0.55 m around the centre, with random orientations. Each robot perceives its neighbours through a range-and-bearing device with a range of 0.36 m: neighbour-count conditions only see robots closer than that. A run lasts 1200 simulation steps (120 s at 10 steps per second).
For reference, the published setup uses 20 robots, an inner radius of 0.87 m and a range of 0.50 m, so thresholds tuned for that setup may need adjusting here.

# 3. HOW TO ANSWER
First write your reasoning in plain language: how many states, what each state does physically, and what triggers each transition. Two or three sentences per machine is enough.
Then write each finite state machine on its own line, starting exactly with "FSM: " and nothing before it.

# 4. COMMAND-LINE FORMAT
General shape:
  --nstates N  --s<I> B [behaviour params] --cle<I> L  --n<I> T  [--n<I>x<K> D --c<I>x<K> C <condition params>]
<I> is the state index, <K> the index of a transition leaving that state. Both start at 0.

## A. Global
* `--nstates N` : number of states, integer in [1..4].

## B. Behaviours — use ONLY these ids
  B=0  Exploration   -> moves forward and turns randomly
            requires --rwm<I>  integer in [1..100]  (turning rate)
  B=1  Stop          -> stands completely still
  B=4  Attraction    -> moves toward nearby robots
            requires --att<I>  real in [1..5]  (attraction gain)
  B=5  Repulsion     -> moves away from nearby robots
            requires --rep<I>  real in [1..5]  (repulsion gain)
  B=6  Spiral        -> traces an expanding spiral
            requires --spd<I>  integer in [1..100]  (spiral speed)
  B=8  GoToColor     -> moves toward the nearest object of a target colour
            requires --vel<I>  fixed value 1.0  (speed)
            requires --clr<I>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (target colour)
  B=9  GoAwayColor   -> moves away from the nearest object of a target colour
            requires --vel<I>  fixed value 1.0  (speed)
            requires --clr<I>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (target colour)
  B=10  FlockCenter   -> moves toward the centre of the group
            requires --fc<I>  integer in [1..100]  (flocking gain)
  B=11  ZigZag        -> moves in a zigzag pattern
            requires --zfr<I>  integer in [1..100]  (zigzag frequency)

Every state also takes an LED colour:
* `--cle<I> L` : one of {0,4,5,6}  (0=off, 1=red, 2=green, 3=blue, 4=yellow, 5=magenta, 6=cyan)

## C. Transitions
* `--n<I> T` : number of transitions leaving state <I>, integer in [1..4].
  Up to 4 transitions may leave the same state, each with a different condition type. A state that reacts to several different situations is often stronger than a state with one transition, and random sampling produces that variety by default.
* `--n<I>x<K> D` : destination, indexed among the OTHER states only.
  With 3 states, leaving state 1 the others are [0, 2]: D=0 targets state 0, D=1 targets state 2.
  With 2 states, leaving state 0 the only other state is state 1, so D must be 0.
* `--c<I>x<K> C` : condition type — use ONLY these ids
  C=0  floor under the robot is BLACK
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=1  floor under the robot is GRAY
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=2  floor under the robot is WHITE
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=3  number of neighbours ABOVE a threshold
            requires --p<I>x<K>  integer in [1..10]  (threshold: an INTEGER count of neighbours, not a probability)
            requires --w<I>x<K>  real in [0..20]  (steepness of the sigmoid)
  C=4  number of neighbours BELOW a threshold
            requires --p<I>x<K>  integer in [1..10]  (threshold: an INTEGER count of neighbours, not a probability)
            requires --w<I>x<K>  real in [0..20]  (steepness of the sigmoid)
  C=5  fires with a fixed probability at every step
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=7  the robot PERCEIVES a given colour with its camera
            requires --l<I>x<K>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (colour to look for)
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])

# 5. HARD RULES
1. Integers stay integers; real-valued parameters always carry a decimal point (1.0, not 1).
2. Every state that exists must declare `--n<I>`, and every declared transition must carry its destination, its condition type and that condition's parameters. A missing parameter crashes the simulator.
3. `--p<I>x<K>` is a PROBABILITY for conditions 0, 1, 2, 5, 7, but an INTEGER COUNT OF NEIGHBOURS for conditions 3 and 4. Never write 0.8 where a neighbour count is expected.
4. Never point a transition at its own state.

# 6. YOU ARE THE EVOLUTIONARY OPERATOR
This is a multi-round conversation on one real ARGoS3 optimisation run. The numerical optimiser is restarted from scratch at the beginning of every round, so exploration and exploitation are entirely up to you.

1. SURVIVAL (elitism): copy the current best-ever machine verbatim in your answer so its lineage is not lost.
2. EXPLOITATION: spend about half of your machines on small edits of the best performers — shift a probability, a threshold or a gain by roughly ten percent, and keep the topology.
3. EXPLORATION: spend the rest on genuinely different topologies or module combinations, to escape local optima.
4. RICHNESS: do not let every state settle on a single transition of the same condition type. Include candidates with two to four transitions per state mixing different condition types.
